L1378: Integrated Summary
L1379: 1) Intestinal absorption: Low apical to basolateral transport and extensive basolateral to apical transport in Caco-2 cells suggest that efflux transporters serve as a rate limiting barrier to
L1380: absorption. The efflux ratio greater than 2 for rifamycin SV prompted the Sponsor to further assess P-gp and BCRP mediated transport with specific probes and inhibitors.
L1381: 2) P-gp substrate: Rifamycin SV appears to be a substrate of P-gp based on a &gt;2-fold increase in the ER and NFR in transporter over-expressing cells compared to wildtype cells. Decreases in ER and NFR in the presence of verapamil (P-gp inhibitor) further support the conclusion.3) BCRP Substrate: Rifamycin SV does not appear to be a substrate for BCRP based on the &lt;2-fold difference in ER between transporter over-expressing and wildtype cells. No significant decreases in ER and NFR in the presence of Ko143 (BCRP inhibitor) further support the conclusion.4) P-gp inhibitor effects on rifamycin SV exposure with Rifamycin SV MMX®: Minimal systemic exposure (bioavailability ca. 0.04%) in conjunction with poor drug solubility, proposed limited treatment duration, and targeted colon release the review team does not expect an interaction and increase in systemic rifamycin SV exposure when concomitantly administered with P-gp inhibitors to be clinically significant.5) P-gp inducer effects on rifamycin SV exposure in the gastrointestinal tract with rifamycin SV MMX®: As mentioned above, rifamycin SV MMX® exerts its actions locally in the lumen therefore risk of treatment failure would not be expected as more drug would be effluxed back to the lumen.

L1450: REVIEWER ASSESSMENT





Experimental Approach (FDA in vitro metabolism and transporter DDI Guidance 2017)
Controls/concentrations appropriate?  Yes  No
Test concentrations appropriate?  Yes  No
Assay System Functional?  Yes  No
Note(s):

1. A plasma C_{max} = 0.012 \mu\text{M} was observed following repeat Rifamycin SV MMX® administration (400 mg b.i.d. for 3 days)
2. Luminal gut concentration estimated at 2293 \mu\text{M} rifamycin SV base (I_{gut} = \text{Dose [molar units]}/250 \text{ mL})
3. IC_{50} is the inhibitor concentration showing 50% inhibition
4. Caco-2 determined rifamycin SV IC_{50} for P-gp is 6.54 \mu\text{M}.
5. MDCKII rifamycin SV IC_{50} for BCRP could not be determined (&gt;34.4 \mu\text{M})





Integrated Summary

1) Inhibition of P-gp transporters due to systemic levels of rifamycin SV: Based on the highest observed mean plasma C_{max} after repeat administration of 400 mg Rifamycin SV MMX®, inhibition of P-gp or BCRP transporters systemically are not likely.
2) Effect of rifamycin SV on P-gp transporters in the gastrointestinal tract: The draft 2017 FDA DDI guidance recommends the following cut off criteria to assess the potential for a drug to inhibit P-gp: I_{gut}/IC_{50} &gt; 10. Based on the reviewer's estimate, an I_{gut}/IC_{50} of ca. 351 suggests that rifamycin SV may be an inhibitor of P-gp transporters in the gut.
3) Effect of rifamycin SV on BCRP transporters in the gastrointestinal tract: The draft 2017 FDA DDI guidance recommends the same cutoff criteria as above to assess the potential for a drug to inhibit BCRP. At the maximum concentration tested (34.4 \mu\text{M}), ca. 38% inhibition was observed. It is unclear from the data whether greater concentrations would result in more inhibition. Therefore, since theoretical concentrations in the lumen of the gastrointestinal tract could reach 2293 \mu\text{M} the review team chose to use 34.4 \mu\text{M} as the IC_{50} (worst-case) to calculate the I_{gut}/IC_{50}. A ratio of 67.7 suggest that rifamycin SV may be an inhibitor of BCRP in the gut.

L1452: REVIEWER ASSESSMENT

Controls/concentrations appropriate?  Yes  No

Test concentrations appropriate?  Yes  No

Assay System Functional?  Yes  No

Note(s):



1. A plasma Cmax = 0.012 \muM was observed following repeat rifamycin SV MMX® administration (400 mg b.i.d. for 3 days)
2. Cmax, unbound = 12 nM x 0.2 (fraction unbound) = 2.4 nM
3. IC50 is the inhibitor concentration showing 50% inhibition



Integrated Summary



1) The draft FDA 2017 guidance indicates that a Cmax,unbound/IC50 &gt;0.1 indicates that the drug may be an inhibitor of renal uptake transporters (OAT1, OAT3, and OCT3). Based on the highest observed plasma Cmax of rifamycin SV after administration of 400 mg rifamycin SV MMX® (~0.012 \muM or 12 nM) and a fraction unbound of 0.20, the unbound Cmax/IC50 is significantly lower than 0.1, hence rifamycin SV is not expected to inhibit OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2 transporters in vivo.
2) The draft FDA 2017 guidance indicates that a Cmax,unbound/IC50 &gt;0.2 indicates that the drug may be an inhibitor of renal uptake transporters (MATE-1 and MATE-2K). Based on the highest observed plasma Cmax of rifamycin SV after administration of 400 mg rifamycin SV MMX® (~0.012 \muM or 12
L1453: nM) and a fraction unbound of 0.20, the unbound C_{max}/IC_{50} is significantly lower than 0.1, hence rifamycin SV is not expected to inhibit MATE-1 or MATE-2K transporters in vivo.
L1454: 3) No in vitro experiments were conducted to determine if rifamycin SV is a substrate of OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, MATE1, or MATE2-K. The review team finds this acceptable as the primary elimination rifamycin SV is not active renal secretion and thus follows recommendations laid out in draft FDA 2017 guidance

L1456: REVIEWER ASSESSMENT
L1457: Integrated Summary
L1458: Acid Suppressant Drug Interaction. Concomitant proton pump inhibitor use was ca. 95% for each treatment arm in the “Drugs for Acid Related Disorders” subgroup. No statistical differences were noted in Clinical Cure Rate. Overall, rifamycin SV is expected to primarily undergo colonic drug release based on the following considerations:
L1459: a) Presence of a film coat and controlled release excipients (biopharm summary, pg.8) to ensure that rifamycin SV is released directly into the colon.
b) Dissolution data where the batch of rifamycin SV RV used for Phase 3 trials and the registration batch showed minimal dissolution at pH1 and pH 7.2 (after 1 hour) (page 11 of the biopharma summary)
c) Available PK data from the BA and FE assessment where most the concentrations above BLQ are observed after 6 hours (which seems to correspond to the dissolution data).
d) Theoretical basis which suggest that the small intestine is the major site of drug absorption1
L1460: Reference:
L1461: 1. http://www.derangedphysiology.com/main/cicm-primary-exam/required-reading/pharmacokinetics/Chapter%201.3.4/drug-absorption-small-intestine
L1462: Reviewer Analysis
L1463: Title: In Vivo Inhibition Potential of the OATP1B1 Transporter by Rifamycin SV MMX®: An In Vitro and In Silico approach.
L1464: Rationale/Background: Rifamycin SV is found on the FDA Interactions and Labeling website as an in vitro inhibitor for the OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 transporters. Furthermore, an article by Acocella et al demonstrated elevated bilirubin following rifamycin SV intravenous administration(Acocella, Nicolis et al. 1965).
L1465: Elevations in total bilirubin was noted in ca. 1-2% of subjects (.I.M5, 5.3.5.3, ISS table 30.1). Because bilirubin is a substrate of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 transporters, the potential for rifamycin SV (after administration of rifamycin SV MMX tablets) to inhibit OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 transporters was assessed as outlined in the draft FDA 2012 DDI guidance.
L1466: Objective: Assess potential clinical significance for a concentration-related interaction between Rifamycin SV MMX® and OATP1B1.
L1467: Methods: A basic static physiologically based PK model for OATP1B1/3 inhibition was utilized using Sponsor and published literature data as recommended by FDA guidance3. Definitions of terms can also be found in FDA guidance3.
L1468: R = 1 + ((f_{u,p} \times I_{in,max})/IC_{50})
L1469: IC_{50} = 2 \mu\text{M} \text{ (Vavricka et al(Vavricka, Van Montfoort et al. 2002))}
L1470: [I]_{in,max} = 0.026 \mu\text{M}
L1471: [I]_{in,max} = (I_{max} + (F_a \times F_g \times K_a \times Dose))/(Q_h/R_b)
L1472: MW = 697.77 \text{ g/mol (Sponsor data)}
L1473: \text{Dose} = 400 \text{ mg (Sponsor data; proposed dose)}
L1474: I_{max} = 0.012 \mu\text{M} \text{ (Sponsor data)}
L1475: K_a = 0.1 \text{ min}^{-1} \text{ (FDA Guidance)}
L1476: F_{u,p} = 0.2 \text{ (Sponsor data)}
L1477: Q_h = 1.62 \text{ L/min (FDA Guidance)}
L1478: R_b = 1 \text{ (FDA Guidance)}
L1479: F_g = 1 \text{ (Estimated from Sponsor data)}
L1480: F_a = 0.004 \text{ (Sponsor data)}
L1481: Model Predictions for Clinical Potential of Drug Inhibition Interactions

AUCR



OATP1B11.00
L1482: Conclusion:
L1483: 1. The estimated maximum hepatic inlet concentration achieved for rifamycin SV MMX® is ca. 0.149 uM and the reported IC_{50} ca. 2 \mu\text{M}; ca. 1 order of magnitude greater (base \text{Log}_{10}). hence, rifamycin SV is unlikely to inhibit OATP transporters in vivo, when given as rifamycin SV MMX® tablet formulation. The model does predict that rifamycin SV may inhibit OATP transporters after intravenous administration and may help to explain, in part, the observation of increases in bilirubin after intravenous administration of rifamycin SV (as described in Acocella et al article).
2. No in vitro studies were conducted by the Sponsor to evaluate rifamycin SV as an OATP1B1 or OATP1B3 substrate. The review team find this acceptable as systemic absorption is (&lt;0.1%) and follows recommendations set forth in FDA guidance3.
L1484: References:
L1485: 3. Food and Drug Administration. 2017. In Vitro Metabolism - and Transporter - Mediated Drug- Drug Interaction Studies Guidance for Industry. [ONLINE] Available at: https://www.fda.gov/downloads/Drugs/Guidances/UCM581965.pdf. [Accessed 14 June 2018]
